> gpu_f64: NVIDIA RTX A6000 has 1/32-1/64 FP64 throughput; its float64 timings are a smoke test, not a production GPU number (see submit_h100.sbatch).

> gpu_f32: float32 dots run in TF32 (JAX default on Ampere+); its accuracy tables measure TF32 rounding (~1e-2..1e-1), not the C^{-1} route.  Read float32 route accuracy from a --matmul-precision highest leg (gpu_f32_hi).

## CORRECTNESS (float64): likelihood part U_var - U_floor
cell = max rel. gradient error vs main / vs reference; [OK|FAIL] is the 1e-11 algebra gate (white, expcos), [ok|concerning|fail] the reference flag (> 1e-8 concerning, > 1e-6 fail).
Normalization: |g - g_ref| / (max over ALL points of max|g_ref[site]|) per site ('cloud') when the JSON has it, else per point (max|g_ref[site]| at the same point, which blows up near the mode where the warmup points sit).

> The two policies differ only in the constants a, atilde, sigma^2 (solve_toeplitz as PR #141 vs longdouble Levinson rounded to f64); the difference is the precompute's forward error (F1), not the FFT route.

## DEVICE TIME PER GRADIENT (us/grad)
Slope method over two fori_loop counts, medians of rep timings; '+-x%' is half the range of the slope over the individual repetitions (repeatability, not a confidence interval), when recorded.

> cpu_f64_omp8 thread configuration: BLAS threads (OMP) = 8, XLA:CPU pool: NPROC = 8, 8 tf_XLAEigen threads.  CPU numbers depend on BOTH: with a multi-thread XLA pool the batched-FFT variants (gs_full, gs_half), the Eigen dots (gemm_*) and the prior-only floor run 2-3x SLOWER at N >= 1024 than with a 1-thread pool while main's trsm gets faster; gs_pr's per-column vmapped FFTs are insensitive.  Production (`import ringdown`: OMP_NUM_THREADS=1, NPROC unset) is the cpu_f64_prod leg; quote CPU speedups with the thread setting.

> cpu_f64_omp8: XLA:CPU hoisted theta-independent work out of the timing loop for gs_pr_ascoded@fast (fft:34->30/fft:51->45); gs_pr_ascoded@pow2 (fft:34->30/fft:51->45); main (trsm:10->8,trsm_big:6->4/trsm:13->10,trsm_big:9->6) (census 'moved' column).  For `main` these are the two N x 1 solves z = L^{-1} y, so on this leg main ~= main_hoisted and 'speedup vs main' excludes the per-gradient recomputation production pays on GPU.  XLA:GPU hoists nothing, so 'speedup vs main' means different things on the two platforms; use 'vs main_hoisted' as the route comparison everywhere.  Inside NUTS tree building the same loop-invariant code motion applies on CPU (z paid once per trajectory).

Production (`import ringdown`: OMP_NUM_THREADS=1, NPROC unset) is the cpu_f64_prod leg; quote CPU speedups with the thread setting.

> cpu_f64_omp1: XLA:CPU hoisted theta-independent work out of the timing loop for gs_pr_ascoded@fast (fft:34->30/fft:51->45); gs_pr_ascoded@pow2 (fft:34->30/fft:51->45); main (trsm:10->8,trsm_big:6->4/trsm:13->10,trsm_big:9->6) (census 'moved' column).  For `main` these are the two N x 1 solves z = L^{-1} y, so on this leg main ~= main_hoisted and 'speedup vs main' excludes the per-gradient recomputation production pays on GPU.  XLA:GPU hoists nothing, so 'speedup vs main' means different things on the two platforms; use 'vs main_hoisted' as the route comparison everywhere.  Inside NUTS tree building the same loop-invariant code motion applies on CPU (z paid once per trajectory).

Production (`import ringdown`: OMP_NUM_THREADS=1, NPROC unset) is the cpu_f64_prod leg; quote CPU speedups with the thread setting.

> cpu_f32_omp8 thread configuration: BLAS threads (OMP) = 8, XLA:CPU pool: NPROC = 8, 8 tf_XLAEigen threads.  CPU numbers depend on BOTH: with a multi-thread XLA pool the batched-FFT variants (gs_full, gs_half), the Eigen dots (gemm_*) and the prior-only floor run 2-3x SLOWER at N >= 1024 than with a 1-thread pool while main's trsm gets faster; gs_pr's per-column vmapped FFTs are insensitive.  Production (`import ringdown`: OMP_NUM_THREADS=1, NPROC unset) is the cpu_f64_prod leg; quote CPU speedups with the thread setting.

> cpu_f32_omp8: XLA:CPU hoisted theta-independent work out of the timing loop for gs_pr_ascoded@fast (fft:34->30/fft:51->45); gs_pr_ascoded@pow2 (fft:34->30/fft:51->45); main (trsm:10->8,trsm_big:6->4/trsm:13->10,trsm_big:9->6) (census 'moved' column).  For `main` these are the two N x 1 solves z = L^{-1} y, so on this leg main ~= main_hoisted and 'speedup vs main' excludes the per-gradient recomputation production pays on GPU.  XLA:GPU hoists nothing, so 'speedup vs main' means different things on the two platforms; use 'vs main_hoisted' as the route comparison everywhere.  Inside NUTS tree building the same loop-invariant code motion applies on CPU (z paid once per trajectory).

> gpu_f64: op counts differ between the plain gradient and the loop body for gemm_cinv (gemm:0->6,dot:12->6); gemm_linv (gemm:0->6,dot:13->7) (census 'moved' column).  On GPU these are cuBLAS-vs-HLO-dot lowering differences inside the body, not hoisting: XLA:GPU keeps main's N x 1 solves and gs_pr_ascoded's constant rffts in the loop, which is why main_hoisted is 4-6% (f64) / 8-19% (f32) faster than main and gs_pr_ascoded ~10% slower than gs_pr here but not on CPU.

Rows sorted by cond(C); 'cloud' = normalized by the point cloud's per-site gradient scale (per point otherwise).  The per-kind table below is the one to read trends from: the warmup (typical-set) points carry a k x k-tail cancellation that the N(0,1) points do not, and several cells (e.g. aligo2 (3,1024,8), the non-gw150914 (2,4096,4) files) have NO warmup points, so the overall column mixes populations.
